<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the rational Hodge conjecture on every self-power of the Jacobian at each tensor Hodge-generic point of the full marked variation of a connected abelian cover of curves. This holds in every base genus and for every compatible branching pattern. For any CM abelian variety, the conclusion also holds for every self-power of its product with the Jacobian, on the same Hodge-generic locus. We also prove the conjecture on every self-power of a very general member of the full smooth labelled family of diagonal complete intersections cut out by at most two equations of a common degree, in every dimension and every degree at least two.

- **claim-cover-jacobian-powers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p4-abelian-covers)): For each fixed connected topological abelian cover as above, every $s\in\mathcal T_\phi^{\mathrm{Hg}}$, every complex CM abelian variety $M$, and every integer $N\geq1$, the rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension of $$\bigl(\operatorname{Jac}(C_s)\times M\bigr)^N.$$ It also holds for $C_s^a\times M^b$ for all integers $a,b\geq0$, where a zeroth power means a point. The point $s$ is chosen before $M$: the same Hodge-generic locus works for every CM companion.
  - Explanation: The same tensor Hodge-generic parameter supports the rational Hodge conjecture on every Jacobian power with any CM companion, and on the indicated products of curves and CM varieties.
- **claim-diagonal-self-powers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Fix $n\geq2$, $d\geq0$, $c\in\{0,1,2\}$, and put $m=d+c+1$. Outside a countable union of proper closed algebraic subsets of $\mathcal D_{c,m}$, the rational Hodge conjecture holds in every codimension on $X_A^u$ for every $u\geq1$. For $c\leq1$, or for $d=0$, no exceptional subset is needed.
  - Explanation: All self-powers satisfy the rational Hodge conjecture outside the stated algebraic exceptional union. The lower-codimension and finite-dimensional endpoints need no exceptional union.
- **claim-companion-algebraic-operations** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-comp-tools)): 1. *CM operations.* CM supply is preserved by external products, permutations, cup products, rational algebraic correspondences, $k$-linear combinations of such correspondences, and scalar conjugation. Every rational Hodge class in a CM-supplied subspace is algebraic. [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Lemma 2.1]. 2. *Admissible operations.* Every admissible map is induced on the indicated summands by a $k$-linear combination of rational algebraic correspondences. Pair tensors are algebraic over $k$, and contraction by the inverse pairing is an algebraic cohomological operation. If $W$ is admissible, then $\overline W$ is admissible and paired with $W$. Alternation and these operations factor determinant lines across admissible direct sums and contract paired determinants nontrivially; an alternating self-pair supplies its top exterior line. [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Lemma 2.2 and its determinant consequences]. 3. *Tensor stabilizer.* Inside the symplectic group of $H$, $\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$ is the pointwise stabilizer of all rational balanced tensors in $H^{\otimes 2a}$, for all $a\geq0$. [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Lemma 2.3]. 4. *Toric summands.* If $H\subset H^1(A,\mathbb Q)$ is a rational Hodge summand of a complex abelian variety and $\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$ is a torus, then $H\simeq H^1(B,\mathbb Q)$ for a CM isogeny factor $B$ of $A$. Its scalar extension decomposes into CM embedding lines, with repeated occurrences distinguished. [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Lemma 2.4].
  - Explanation: The companion operations preserve CM supply, realize admissible maps algebraically, identify the Hodge group through balanced tensors, and realize toric summands by CM isogeny factors.
- **claim-saturated-determinant-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-comp-saturated)): Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety, $H=H^1(A,\mathbb Q)$, $J=\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$, and $R=J^{\mathrm{der}}$. The following data are called saturated: 1. a rational Hodge summand $H_0\subset H$ with toric Hodge group, together with a decomposition of $H_{0,k}$ into CM embedding lines, with repeated occurrences distinguished; 2. a decomposition of the complement of $H_{0,k}$ into blocks of nonzero admissible spaces, each admissibly identified with an actual representative $V_i$ carrying an alternating or symmetric self-pair, or, in a linear block with $\dim V_i\geq2$, with $V_i$ or $\overline V_i$, paired with each other; in every block $V_i$ is chosen as an actual summand of that block; 3. containment in the image of $R_k$ of the product $$\prod_{i\in I_{\mathrm{Sp}}}\operatorname{Sp}(V_i) \times\prod_{i\in I_{\mathrm{SO}}}\operatorname{SO}(V_i) \times\prod_{i\in I_{\mathrm{SL}}}\operatorname{SL}(V_i),$$ acting standardly on its own copies, dually on the opposite linear copies, and trivially on all other blocks and on $H_{0,k}$; 4. CM supply for every singleton $D(V_i)$ in an orthogonal or linear block, without a balance condition. The symplectic and orthogonal forms in <span class="nodecor">(c)</span> are inverse to the specified pair tensors. The pair in a linear block identifies $\overline V_i$ with $V_i^*(-1)$ as a Hodge-graded space and with the dual as a group representation. Saturated data imply that every rational balanced tensor is algebraic on its separate $A$-slots and that the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every self-power of $A$. For every CM abelian variety $M$, the induced data on $A\times M$ remain saturated and give the same conclusions on all its powers. This is the saturated clause of [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Theorem 2.5].
  - Explanation: Saturated data combine toric summands, admissibly linked standard blocks, independent derived actions, and separate determinant supply. They imply algebraicity of balanced tensors and persist after adjoining every CM companion.
- **claim-correspondence-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-comp-transfer)): Let $\mathscr S$ be a collection of smooth projective varieties such that the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every finite product of its members. Suppose that, for each smooth projective $Y_i$ in a finite list and each degree $q$, the group $H^q(Y_i,\mathbb Q)$ is spanned by maps $$\Gamma_*:H^{q-2j}(S,\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow H^q(Y_i,\mathbb Q), \qquad \Gamma\in\operatorname{CH}^{\dim S+j}(S\times Y_i)_\mathbb Q,\quad S\in\mathscr S .$$ Then the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every finite product of the $Y_i$, with repetitions and with additional members of $\mathscr S$. The same holds for restrictions of maps from $k$-linear combinations of rational algebraic correspondences if their images span the full target degree after scalar extension. [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Lemma 2.6].
  - Explanation: Spanning whole target cohomology degrees by algebraic source maps transfers the finite-product rational Hodge conjecture, including repetitions and additional source factors.
- **claim-finite-quotient-cohomology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-quotient)): If a finite group $G$ acts algebraically on a projective variety $V$, the quotient map $q:V\to V/G$ induces $$\label{p4:finite-quotient} q^*:H^i(V/G,\mathbb Q)\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}H^i(V,\mathbb Q)^G.$$ The assertion remains true after extending coefficients. Pullback is a morphism of mixed Hodge structures; if $V$ is smooth and projective, the cohomology of $V/G$ is pure of the indicated degree.
  - Explanation: Finite quotient pullback identifies rational quotient cohomology with invariant cohomology and respects mixed Hodge structures. Smooth projective sources give pure quotient cohomology.
- **claim-bundle-blowup-supply** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-p4-bundle-blowup-supply)): If the full cohomology of a smooth projective variety $S$ is CM-supplied, the same holds for every projective bundle over $S$. If $Z\subset X$ is a nonempty smooth closed subvariety of pure codimension $c\ge1$ in a smooth projective variety, and the full cohomologies of $X$ and $Z$ are CM-supplied, the same holds for $\operatorname{Bl}_Z X$. In both assertions finite correspondence supplies for the inputs give finite supplies for the output in each degree.
  - Explanation: Projective bundles and blowups preserve full CM supply under the stated smoothness and input-supply hypotheses, with finite supplies in each degree.
- **claim-fermat-full-cohomology-supply** (construction, [source](#stmt-p4-fermat-supply)): For integers $n\ge2$ and $a\ge0$, let $$F_n^a=\{x_0^n+\cdots+x_{a+1}^n=0\} \subset\mathbb P^{a+1}_\mathbb C.$$ For every degree $q$, there are finitely many CM abelian varieties $B_\nu$, integers $j_\nu$, and rational algebraic correspondences $\Gamma_\nu\in\operatorname{CH}^{\dim B_\nu+j_\nu}(B_\nu\times F_n^a)_\mathbb Q$ such that the sum of their maps $$\bigoplus_\nu H^{q-2j_\nu}(B_\nu,\mathbb Q)(-j_\nu) \xrightarrow{\;\sum_\nu(\Gamma_\nu)_*\;} H^q(F_n^a,\mathbb Q)$$ is surjective. One may take every $B_\nu$ to be a power of the CM abelian variety $\operatorname{Jac}(F_n^1)$, allowing its zeroth power, a point. In dimension zero the finite reduced scheme $F_n^0$ is supplied component by component by points.
  - Explanation: Every Fermat cohomology degree is supplied by finitely many rational correspondences from powers of the same Fermat-curve Jacobian, including point sources.
- **claim-faithful-cyclic-determinants** (construction, [source](#stmt-p4-cyclic-determinant)): Let $q:C\to B$ be a connected cyclic cover of smooth connected projective complex curves, with group $\Delta$ of order $m\ge2$. Let $\chi:\Delta\to k^\times$ be faithful and put $V_\chi=H^1(C,k)_\chi$, using inverse pullbacks for the group action. Then $D(V_\chi)$ is CM-supplied on its product of curve slots. The assertion includes $V_\chi=0$.* *More precisely, the CM sources in the defining correspondence span may all be taken to be powers of the fixed CM abelian variety $\operatorname{Jac}(F_m^1)$, allowing its zeroth power, a point.
  - Explanation: A faithful cyclic character determinant is supplied on separate curve slots from powers of a fixed Fermat-curve Jacobian. The assertion includes the unit determinant of a zero character space.
- **claim-abelian-character-determinants** (construction, [source](#stmt-p4-abelian-determinants)): Let a finite abelian group $P$ act faithfully on a smooth connected projective complex curve $C$, and put $B=C/P$. For every character $\chi:P\to k^\times$, the line $D(H^1(C,k)_\chi)$ is CM-supplied. This includes the zero character spaces. For the trivial character the line is generated by a rational algebraic cycle class.* *For $\chi\ne1$, let $r_\chi$ be the number of branch points of $C/\ker\chi\to B$. Then $$\dim_k H^1(C,k)_\chi=2g(B)+r_\chi-2.$$ The number $r_\chi$ counts precisely the original branch points whose inertia is not killed by $\chi$.* *For a fixed $P$, the CM sources for all these lines can be taken to be powers of one fixed product of Fermat-curve Jacobians depending only on $P$, not on the complex structure of $C$.
  - Explanation: Cyclic quotient pullback supplies every abelian character determinant. The invariant determinant is algebraic, and a fixed product of Fermat-curve Jacobians supplies all characters independently of the complex structure.
- **claim-marked-tensor-genericity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p4-generic-tensors)): For $a\ge0$ and $t\in H_\mathbb Q^{\otimes 2a}$, let $$Z_t=\{s\in\mathcal T_\phi: t_\mathbb C\in F^a(H_\mathbb C^{\otimes 2a})_s\},\qquad \mathcal T_\phi^{\mathrm{Hg}} =\mathcal T_\phi\setminus \bigcup_{\substack{a\ge0,\ t\in H_\mathbb Q^{\otimes 2a}\\ Z_t\ne\mathcal T_\phi}}Z_t.$$ Each $Z_t$ is closed complex analytic. The complement $\mathcal T_\phi^{\mathrm{Hg}}$ is nonempty and is the complement of a countable union of proper closed analytic subsets. At a point in this complement, every rational balanced tensor is balanced throughout $\mathcal T_\phi$.* *Here $H_s$ denotes $H_\mathbb Q$ with the Hodge structure at $s$. Let $J_s=\operatorname{Hdg}(H_s)$ and let $L$ be the rational Zariski closure of $\Gamma$ in the symplectic group of $H_\mathbb Q$. At every $s\in\mathcal T_\phi^{\mathrm{Hg}}$ one has $L^\circ\subset J_s$, and the whole $\Gamma$ normalizes $J_s$.
  - Explanation: Removing proper analytic tensor Hodge loci leaves a nonempty locus where balanced rational tensors are global. Connected monodromy lies in the Hodge group there, and the full mapping-class stabilizer normalizes it.
- **claim-cover-saturated-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p4-cover-saturation)): For every connected topological abelian cover and full marked family defined above, and every $s\in\mathcal T_\phi^{\mathrm{Hg}}$, the Jacobian $\operatorname{Jac}(C_s)$ admits saturated data in Companion results <a href="#comp:saturated" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:saturated">4</a>.
  - Explanation: At every tensor Hodge-generic point of the full marked cover family, the Jacobian has the saturated data required by the companion determinant criterion.
- **claim-rank-one-standard-actions** (classification, [source](#stmt-p4-cover-rank-one)): Let $V$ be a finite-dimensional vector space over $k=\overline{\mathbb Q}$, and let a connected reductive algebraic subgroup of $\operatorname{GL}(V)$ act irreducibly. If its Lie algebra contains a rank-one nilpotent, its derived image is the standard special linear or standard symplectic group on $V$.
  - Explanation: A rank-one nilpotent in an irreducible connected reductive action forces the derived image to be standard special linear or standard symplectic.
- **claim-cocycle-twist-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-p4-twist-model)): For a nontrivial $t$, the character space is the space of crossed homomorphisms $$\label{p4:cocycles} z(uv)=z(u)+t(u)z(v), \qquad z(u)\sim z(u)+(1-t(u))b\quad(b\in k).$$ Use generators $a_j,b_j$ for the handles and positively based meridians $g_i$, with relation $\prod_j[a_j,b_j]\prod_i g_i=1$. Values of $z$ on these generators satisfy just the crossed-homomorphism relation on this word, followed by the one-dimensional coboundary quotient.* *Let $\alpha$ be an embedded arc between two active punctures. A twist about the boundary of its two-puncture disk acts, in adapted coordinates and after choosing one of the two twist conventions, by $$\label{p4:pair-twist} T_\alpha=1+u_\alpha\ell_\alpha,\qquad u_\alpha=(-t_i,1),\qquad \ell_\alpha(z)=(1-t_j)z(g_i)+(t_i-1)z(g_j),$$ with $u_\alpha$ supported on these two meridians. Its exceptional eigenvalue is $t_it_j$. A different choice of lift can multiply this formula by a finite-order scalar.* *For a nontrivial such transformation, its fixed hyperplane is the Hermitian orthogonal complement of its image direction. If a collection of these directions has connected nonorthogonality graph and their fixed hyperplanes have zero intersection, the group they generate acts irreducibly. These statements apply also to ordinary transvections arising from simple closed curves with trivial $t$-monodromy.
  - Explanation: Crossed homomorphisms model nontrivial character cohomology. Disk twists have rank-one displacement, and connected nonorthogonality together with a trivial common fixed space gives irreducibility.
- **claim-connected-rank-one-irreducibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p4-connected-irreducibility)): Suppose a subgroup of $\operatorname{GL}(V)$ acts irreducibly and is generated, up to scalars, by rank-one changes of the identity. If the Lie algebra of its Zariski closure contains a rank-one nilpotent, the identity component of that closure acts irreducibly.
  - Explanation: Irreducibility for a group generated up to scalars by rank-one changes passes to its identity component when the closure's Lie algebra contains a rank-one nilpotent.
- **claim-handle-winding-arcs** (construction, [source](#stmt-p4-winding-arcs)): Place the active punctures in an ordered disk, away from the handle subsurface. For every simple based handle generator $x$, an embedded arc from puncture $1$ to puncture $2$ can be chosen whose transported fixed-point condition is Equation <a href="#p4:fixed-points" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="p4:fixed-points">[p4:fixed-points]</a> with $x$ or $x^{-1}$. If $r\ge3$, the arc can avoid the interior of the standard arc from $2$ to $3$. Arcs for different generators are chosen separately; they are not required to be mutually disjoint.* *For a winding arc using only the last handle, the simple generators of the first $h-1$ handles can be represented outside its two-puncture disk.
  - Explanation: Separate embedded winding arcs realize the transported fixed-point equations for handle generators, with the avoidance properties needed in the twist argument.
- **claim-positive-genus-projections** (classification, [source](#stmt-p4-positive-projections)): For $h\ge1$, every nonzero nontrivial character space is mixed in Hodge type at every scalar conjugate. At every $s\in\mathcal T_\phi^{\mathrm{Hg}}$ the image of the derived Hodge group on that space is the full standard special linear or full standard symplectic group. The invariant character space, when nonzero, has Hodge dimensions $(h,h)$ and full standard symplectic derived action.
  - Explanation: Positive base genus forces each nonzero nontrivial character space to be mixed at every scalar conjugate and gives a standard derived Hodge projection at tensor Hodge-generic points. The invariant character has symplectic derived action.
- **claim-sphere-finite-determinant-orbits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p4-sphere-determinant-finite)): The determinant character of $M_\chi$ has finite image. If the monodromy on one scalar character space is finite, the monodromy on the rational sum of its scalar orbit is finite as well. If the Hermitian forms on every member of a scalar orbit are definite, the monodromy on that rational sum is finite.
  - Explanation: Sphere character monodromy has finite determinant image. Finiteness propagates across the rational scalar orbit, and definiteness at all scalar translates also implies finite monodromy.
- **claim-finite-sphere-monodromy-purity** (classification, [source](#stmt-p4-sphere-finite-pure)): Suppose $n\ge2$. If $M_\chi$ is finite, every scalar translate of $W_\chi$ is pure at every fiber. At every fiber its rational Hodge group acts through scalar operators on each character space in that orbit, and the action on their rational sum is toric.
  - Explanation: Finite sphere monodromy in the stated rank regime forces purity at every scalar translate and every fiber, with scalar character actions and a toric action on the rational orbit.
- **claim-infinite-sphere-connected-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p4-sphere-connected-irreducible)): Suppose $n\ge3$. If $M_\chi$ is infinite, its identity component acts irreducibly on $W_\chi$. Moreover its Lie algebra contains a rank-one nilpotent.
  - Explanation: Infinite sphere character monodromy in the stated rank regime has an irreducible identity-component action and a rank-one nilpotent in its Lie algebra.
- **claim-sphere-standard-projections** (classification, [source](#stmt-p4-sphere-projections)): At every $s\in\mathcal T_\phi^{\mathrm{Hg}}$ the following assertions hold.* 1. *If $n\ge3$, then either $M_\chi$ is finite and the Hodge action on its rational scalar orbit is toric, or $$M_\chi^\circ= \begin{cases} \operatorname{Sp}(W_\chi),&\chi^2=1,\\ \operatorname{SL}(W_\chi),&\chi^2\ne1. \end{cases}$$ In the infinite case the derived Hodge projection is the same standard group.* 2. *In dimensions at most two every non-toric character space has dimension two and has standard derived Hodge projection $\operatorname{SL}_2$.* *Here the symplectic group is for the restricted polarization on the order-two character space. Each non-toric character space is supported by a single simple ideal of $\operatorname{Lie}(J^{\mathrm{der}})_k$.
  - Explanation: Sphere character spaces have toric finite-monodromy alternatives or standard special linear and symplectic projections. The low-rank non-toric case is standard, and each non-toric space has a unique supporting simple ideal.
- **claim-four-point-hodge-signatures** (classification, [source](#stmt-p4-sphere-four-signatures)): Suppose $r=4$ and write $t_i=\exp(2\pi i v_i)$ with $0<v_i<1$. Set $S=\sum_i v_i\in\{1,2,3\}$. In a fixed choice of character convention the two Hodge multiplicities are $S-1$ and $3-S$. Thus $S=2$ means mixed, while $S=1$ and $S=3$ give the two opposite pure types.
  - Explanation: The sum of normalized local exponents determines the two Hodge multiplicities. The middle sum gives mixed type, while the extreme sums give opposite pure orientations.
- **claim-within-sphere-character-links** (classification, [source](#stmt-p4-sphere-links)): Suppose two non-toric character spaces $W_\chi,W_{\chi'}$ are supported by the same simple Hodge Lie ideal. They have the same dimension $n$ and standard derived representations equivalent up to duality.* 1. *If $n\ge3$, then $\chi'=\chi$ or $\chi^{-1}$. The inverse character is identified by the polarization with the polarized dual. If the standard group is symplectic, the character has order two and the space is mixed at every scalar translate.* 2. *If $n=2$, one can choose either $W_\chi$ or its polarized dual as model so that an equivalence of its derived representation with $W_{\chi'}$ respects the Hodge circle at every scalar translate.
  - Explanation: Spaces sharing a simple Hodge ideal have equivalent standard representations up to duality. In higher rank this identifies the character up to inversion; in the remaining non-toric rank a fixed model or polarized dual respects all scalar-translated Hodge circles.
- **claim-mixed-standard-hodge-links** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p4-mixed-links)): Let two admissible irreducible spaces in the same polarized weight-one structure have equivalent representations of a single supporting simple ideal of the derived Hodge Lie algebra. Suppose both spaces are mixed at every simultaneous scalar conjugate. An intertwiner between them is an admissible Hodge isomorphism. The analogous dual comparison is obtained by replacing one space by its opposite paired space.* *If the derived action on one such space is standard symplectic, its invariant alternating tensor is a pair tensor on that space itself.
  - Explanation: An intertwiner between equivalent standard spaces that are mixed at every simultaneous scalar conjugate is admissible. The symplectic invariant alternating tensor is a pair tensor under the stated hypotheses.
- **claim-exact-diagonal-smooth-locus** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-smooth-locus)): Let $n\geq2$, $1\leq c\leq m-1$, and let $A$ be a rank-$c$ matrix with $m$ columns. Then $X_A$ is a complete intersection of dimension $m-c-1$. It is smooth if and only if every $c$-column minor of $A$ is nonzero. This condition is equivalent to coordinate generality of $P=\ker A$.
  - Explanation: For the stated diagonal coefficient matrices, nonvanishing of every maximal column minor is exactly smoothness and exactly coordinate generality of the kernel.
- **claim-nonmiddle-ambient-cohomology** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-ambient)): Let $X_A$ be a smooth diagonal complete intersection as in Proposition <a href="#prop:smooth-locus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:smooth-locus">26</a>, of dimension $d\geq1$, and put $h=c_1(\mathcal O_{X_A}(1))$. For $0\leq k\leq2d$ and $k\ne d$, $$H^k(X_A,\mathbb Q)= \begin{cases} \mathbb Qh^{k/2},&k\text{ even},\\ 0,&k\text{ odd}. \end{cases}$$ In particular $X_A$ is connected.
  - Explanation: Outside the middle degree, odd rational cohomology vanishes and even rational cohomology is generated by hyperplane powers. Positive-dimensional smooth intersections are connected.
- **claim-root-cover-geometry** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-geometry)): For $P\in\mathcal U_{d,m}$, $X_P$ is a smooth projective complete intersection of dimension $d$, connected if $d\geq1$. The map $q_P$ is finite and is the quotient by $G_{m,n}$. The coordinate hyperplanes on $\mathbb P(P)$ and their reduced inverse images on $X_P$ have simple normal crossings. For $d=0$, $X_P$ is a $G_{m,n}$-torsor of reduced points.* *The open $\mathcal U_{d,m}$ is nonempty and connected, and the $X_P$ form a smooth projective family over it with its phase action.
  - Explanation: Coordinate-general root covers are smooth projective complete intersections with finite phase quotient and normal-crossing coordinate boundary. They form a connected smooth parameter family, including finite torsors at the endpoint.
- **claim-evaluation-curve-quotient** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-evaluation)): Choose distinct $a_1,\ldots,a_m\in\mathbb C$, $m\geq3$, and put $$P_j=\{(f(a_1),\ldots,f(a_m)):\deg f\leq j\} \quad(1\leq j\leq m-2),\qquad C=X_{P_1}.$$ Coordinate multiplication defines a finite surjective morphism $$\mu_j:C^j\to X_{P_j},\qquad (x^{(1)},\ldots,x^{(j)})\mapsto [\prod_{\nu=1}^j x_i^{(\nu)}]_{i=1}^m,$$ which identifies the target with $$C^j\big/\left(\ker(G_{m,n}^j\xrightarrow{\prod}G_{m,n}) \rtimes\mathfrak S_j\right).$$ For $b\ne0$, pullback identifies $$H^j(X_{P_j},\overline{\mathbb Q})_b\simeq\bigwedge^j V_b,\qquad V_b=H^1(C,\overline{\mathbb Q})_b,$$ with the exterior power embedded by antisymmetrization.
  - Explanation: Coordinate multiplication realizes evaluation root covers as finite quotients of powers of one curve. Nontrivial character pullback becomes the alternating exterior-power summand.
- **claim-support-character-cohomology** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-characters)): For $P\in\mathcal U_{d,m}$, invariant cohomology is the pullback of $H^*(\mathbb P(P),\overline{\mathbb Q})$ and is spanned by algebraic hyperplane classes. Every nontrivial character occurs only in degree $d$, and $$\dim_{\overline{\mathbb Q}} W_{P,b}=\binom{s(b)-2}{d}\quad(b\ne0),$$ where the value is zero if $s(b)-2<d$.* *If $S=S(b)$ has $s\geq d+2$ elements, projection identifies $P$ with a coordinate-general plane $P_S\subset\mathbb C^S$ and defines a finite quotient $p_S:X_P\to X_{P_S}$. Its pullback is an isomorphism $H^d(X_{P_S},\overline{\mathbb Q})_{b\|_S}\simeq W_{P,b}$. These descriptions commute with smooth parameter pullback.
  - Explanation: Phase-invariant cohomology is ambient and algebraic. Nontrivial characters occur only in the middle degree with the stated support-dependent rank, and projection to a sufficiently large support preserves their cohomology.
- **claim-perfect-gale-tensor** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-gale)): Let $P\in\mathcal U_{d,m}$, set $$Q=P^\perp\quad\text{for }\sum_i u_i v_i,\qquad d'=m-d-2.$$ Then $Q\in\mathcal U_{d',m}$, and $$f_P:X_P\times X_Q\longrightarrow F_n^{m-2},\qquad ([x_i],[y_i])\longmapsto[x_i y_i]$$ is an algebraic morphism. For every full-support character $b$, $L_b=H^{m-2}(F_n^{m-2},\overline{\mathbb Q})_b$ is a line, and pullback maps it to $W_{P,b}\otimes W_{Q,b}$. For every $0\ne\ell\in L_b$, the tensor $f_P^*\ell$ has full pairing rank: the associated map $W_{P,b}^*\to W_{Q,b}$ is an isomorphism. This includes $d=0$ and $d'=0$.
  - Explanation: Bilinear orthogonal complement preserves coordinate generality. Coordinate multiplication pulls a full-support Fermat character line back to a perfect tensor between the complementary middle-degree character spaces, including finite endpoints.
- **claim-forward-gale-cohomology-supply** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-supply)): Let $P\in\mathcal U_{d,m}$. For each $S\subset\{1,\ldots,m\}$ with $s=\|S\|\geq d+2$, put $$P_S=\operatorname{pr}_S P,\quad Q_S=P_S^\perp,\quad Y_S=X_{Q_S}, \quad h_S=s-d-2.$$ There is a rational algebraic correspondence from $F_n^{s-2}\times Y_S$ to $X_P$, with degree shift $-2h_S$, whose image after scalar extension contains $W_{P,b}$ for every character with support $S$.* *Together with hyperplane cycles from a point, these finitely many maps span the full rational cohomology of $X_P$. In the middle degree the source maps may be taken on the entire groups $$H^{d+2h_S}(F_n^{s-2}\times Y_S,\mathbb Q)(h_S).$$
  - Explanation: Graph correspondences from Fermat factors and supported Gale duals span the whole rational target cohomology with the stated degree shifts. They act on entire source degrees rather than requiring algebraic character projectors.
- **claim-low-codimension-dual-sources** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-one-curve)): Let $X_P$ have dimension $d$ and codimension $c=m-d-1\in\{1,2\}$, and write $S=S(b)$ for the support of a character $b$.* 1. *If $c=1$, every nontrivial character $b$ with $W_{P,b}\ne0$ has full support, and its dual source $Y_S$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:supply" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:supply">32</a> is finite.* 2. *If $c=2$, every nontrivial character $b$ with $W_{P,b}\ne0$ has support of size $m$ or $m-1$. The full-support source is the one connected curve $C=X_{P^\perp}$. If $S=\{1,\ldots,m\}\setminus\{i\}$, then $Y_S$ is the reduced $i$th boundary fiber of $C$, with the zero coordinate omitted, and consists of $n^{m-2}$ points.
  - Explanation: A contributing character in the stated codimension range has a finite dual source or the single full-support dual curve. Missing-coordinate supports give reduced boundary fibers of that curve.
- **claim-branch-configuration-product** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-parameter)): The normalized branch configuration is the regular morphism $$\lambda:\mathcal D_{2,m}\to M_m,\qquad z_i=\frac{\Delta_{2i}\Delta_{13}}{\Delta_{1i}\Delta_{23}} \quad(i\geq4).$$ There is a global product isomorphism $\mathcal D_{2,m}\simeq M_m\times(\mathbb G_m)^{m-1}$ under which $\lambda$ is projection. It is smooth and surjective, and the quotient by the effective coefficient torus $(\mathbb G_m)^m/\mathbb G_m$ is $M_m$.
  - Explanation: Minor ratios define the normalized ordered branch map. The coefficient locus splits globally as branch configurations times a torus, and the branch map is smooth and surjective.
- **claim-normalized-root-family-deck-datum** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-cover-family)): The map $$\rho:\widetilde\mathcal D_{2,m,n}:=M_m\times(\mathbb G_m)^{m-1}\to\mathcal D_{2,m}, \qquad (z,h_2,\ldots,h_m)\mapsto(z,h_2^n,\ldots,h_m^n)$$ is a connected finite étale cover of degree $n^{m-1}$. Set $h_1=1$. Over it, the algebraic maps $[y_i]\mapsto[h_i y_i]$ and $[x_i]\mapsto[h_i^{-1}x_i]$ identify the normalized dual and original root families with the families for $Q(t,z)$ and $P(t,z)$, respectively. They commute with $G_{m,n}$.* *Every $q_z$ is a connected $G_{m,n}$-cover of $\mathbb P^1$, with ordered branch points $(\infty,0,1,z_4,\ldots,z_m)$. Identify $$G_{m,n}\simeq(\mathbb Z/n)^m/\langle(1,\ldots,1)\rangle$$ using $\zeta_n=\exp(2\pi i/n)$. Its ordered positive local monodromies are the images $(\bar e_1,\ldots,\bar e_m)$ of the standard basis. The family runs over all ordered branch configurations with this fixed deck datum.
  - Explanation: A finite étale coordinate-root cover gives phase-equivariant algebraic family identifications. The normalized curves run through every ordered branch configuration with the fixed abelian local monodromy tuple.
- **claim-unmarked-sphere-isotopy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sphere-isotopy)): Every orientation-preserving self-homeomorphism of $S^2$ is isotopic to the identity through self-homeomorphisms, with no marked points required to stay fixed during the isotopy.
  - Explanation: Orientation-preserving sphere homeomorphisms admit an isotopy to the identity when intermediate marked positions are allowed to move.
- **claim-based-marked-root-comparison** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-based)): The map $\beta:B_{m,n}\to V_m$ defined above is a connected finite étale $G_{m,n}$-torsor. Pullback of the root family to $B_{m,n}$ has the tautological chosen lift $y$ of the auxiliary basepoint $b=q_z(y)$.* *Use orientation-preserving homeomorphism markings, up to isotopy relative to the labels. Fix an initial based equivariant identification with a root fiber. For every marked complex sphere with the datum of Proposition <a href="#prop:cover-family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cover-family">36</a>, there is a path in $V_m$, lifted from the chosen point of $B_{m,n}$, such that under its induced cohomological identification the endpoint root Hodge filtration is exactly the filtration of the prescribed marked cover. Consequently, a rational tensor which remains Hodge after root-family transport along every path in $M_m$ is Hodge in every marked fiber.* *The parameter space making both coordinate-root and based-lift choices is $$\widetilde\mathcal D_{2,m,n}\times_{M_m}B_{m,n} \simeq B_{m,n}\times(\mathbb G_m)^{m-1}.$$ It is connected and projects smoothly and surjectively to $B_{m,n}$.
  - Explanation: The based-lift parameter space is a connected finite étale phase torsor. A lifted configuration path reaches the filtration of every prescribed marked cover, so globally transported root tensors are Hodge in every marked fiber.
- **claim-algebraic-tensor-genericity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-generic)): Let $S$ be a smooth connected complex algebraic variety carrying an integral polarized variation $\mathbb V$ of weight one. On the universal cover of $S^{\rm an}$, mark its underlying local system. Outside a countable union of proper closed algebraic subsets of $S$, every rational tensor in $\mathbb V_s^{\otimes 2a}$ of type $(a,a)$ remains of that type after parallel transport along every path starting at $s$, for every $a\geq0$.
  - Explanation: For an integral polarized weight-one variation on a smooth connected algebraic base, a very general rational balanced tensor remains Hodge after transport along every path.
- **claim-good-dual-curve-powers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-good-curves)): There is a countable union $Z$ of proper closed algebraic subsets of $M_m$ such that for $z\notin Z$, every CM abelian $B$, and every $N\geq1$, the rational Hodge conjecture holds on $(\operatorname{Jac}(X_{Q_0(z)})\times B)^N$. The same holds for the dual curve at every point of $\mathcal D_{2,m}\setminus\lambda^{-1}(Z)$.
  - Explanation: A single algebraic exceptional union in branch-configuration space suffices for every power and every CM companion. Its pullback controls the dual curves throughout the smooth coefficient locus.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-comp-tools",
        "exact_quote": "1.  *CM operations.* CM supply is preserved by external products, permutations, cup products, rational algebraic correspondences, $k$-linear combinations of such correspondences, and scalar conjugation. Every rational Hodge class in a CM-supplied subspace is algebraic. [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Lemma 2.1].\n\n2.  *Admissible operations.* Every admissible map is induced on the indicated summands by a $k$-linear combination of rational algebraic correspondences. Pair tensors are algebraic over $k$, and contraction by the inverse pairing is an algebraic cohomological operation. If $W$ is admissible, then $\\overline W$ is admissible and paired with $W$. Alternation and these operations factor determinant lines across admissible direct sums and contract paired determinants nontrivially; an alternating self-pair supplies its top exterior line. [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Lemma 2.2 and its determinant consequences].\n\n3.  *Tensor stabilizer.* Inside the symplectic group of $H$, $\\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$ is the pointwise stabilizer of all rational balanced tensors in $H^{\\otimes 2a}$, for all $a\\geq0$. [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Lemma 2.3].\n\n4.  *Toric summands.* If $H\\subset H^1(A,\\mathbb Q)$ is a rational Hodge summand of a complex abelian variety and $\\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$ is a torus, then $H\\simeq H^1(B,\\mathbb Q)$ for a CM isogeny factor $B$ of $A$. Its scalar extension decomposes into CM embedding lines, with repeated occurrences distinguished. [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Lemma 2.4].",
        "source_id": "PM-032.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-comp-saturated",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a complex abelian variety, $H=H^1(A,\\mathbb Q)$, $J=\\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$, and $R=J^{\\mathrm{der}}$. The following data are called saturated:\n\n1.  a rational Hodge summand $H_0\\subset H$ with toric Hodge group, together with a decomposition of $H_{0,k}$ into CM embedding lines, with repeated occurrences distinguished;\n\n2.  a decomposition of the complement of $H_{0,k}$ into blocks of nonzero admissible spaces, each admissibly identified with an actual representative $V_i$ carrying an alternating or symmetric self-pair, or, in a linear block with $\\dim V_i\\geq2$, with $V_i$ or $\\overline V_i$, paired with each other; in every block $V_i$ is chosen as an actual summand of that block;\n\n3.  containment in the image of $R_k$ of the product $$\\prod_{i\\in I_{\\mathrm{Sp}}}\\operatorname{Sp}(V_i)\n     \\times\\prod_{i\\in I_{\\mathrm{SO}}}\\operatorname{SO}(V_i)\n     \\times\\prod_{i\\in I_{\\mathrm{SL}}}\\operatorname{SL}(V_i),$$ acting standardly on its own copies, dually on the opposite linear copies, and trivially on all other blocks and on $H_{0,k}$;\n\n4.  CM supply for every singleton $D(V_i)$ in an orthogonal or linear block, without a balance condition.\n\nThe symplectic and orthogonal forms in <span class=\"nodecor\">(c)</span> are inverse to the specified pair tensors. The pair in a linear block identifies $\\overline V_i$ with $V_i^*(-1)$ as a Hodge-graded space and with the dual as a group representation. Saturated data imply that every rational balanced tensor is algebraic on its separate $A$-slots and that the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every self-power of $A$. For every CM abelian variety $M$, the induced data on $A\\times M$ remain saturated and give the same conclusions on all its powers. This is the saturated clause of [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Theorem 2.5].",
        "source_id": "PM-032.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-comp-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathscr S$ be a collection of smooth projective varieties such that the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every finite product of its members. Suppose that, for each smooth projective $Y_i$ in a finite list and each degree $q$, the group $H^q(Y_i,\\mathbb Q)$ is spanned by maps $$\\Gamma_*:H^{q-2j}(S,\\mathbb Q)\\longrightarrow H^q(Y_i,\\mathbb Q),\n \\qquad\n \\Gamma\\in\\operatorname{CH}^{\\dim S+j}(S\\times Y_i)_\\mathbb Q,\\quad S\\in\\mathscr S .$$ Then the rational Hodge conjecture holds on every finite product of the $Y_i$, with repetitions and with additional members of $\\mathscr S$. The same holds for restrictions of maps from $k$-linear combinations of rational algebraic correspondences if their images span the full target degree after scalar extension. [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Lemma 2.6].",
        "source_id": "PM-032.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-comp-transfer",

The same holds for restrictions of maps from $k$-linear combinations of rational algebraic correspondences if their images span the full target degree after scalar extension. [[OpenAIAbelianPowers](#ref-OpenAIAbelianPowers), Lemma 2.6].",
          "source_id": "PM-032.4"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-quotient",
        "exact_quote": "If a finite group $G$ acts algebraically on a projective variety $V$, the quotient map $q:V\\to V/G$ induces $$\\label{p4:finite-quotient}\n q^*:H^i(V/G,\\mathbb Q)\\xrightarrow{\\;\\sim\\;}H^i(V,\\mathbb Q)^G.$$ The assertion remains true after extending coefficients. Pullback is a morphism of mixed Hodge structures; if $V$ is smooth and projective, the cohomology of $V/G$ is pure of the indicated degree.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-quotient",
          "exact_quote": "If a finite group $G$ acts algebraically on a projective variety $V$, the quotient map $q:V\\to V/G$ induces $$\\label{p4:finite-quotient}\n q^*:H^i(V/G,\\mathbb Q)\\xrightarrow{\\;\\sim\\;}H^i(V,\\mathbb Q)^G.$$ The assertion remains true after extending coefficients. Pullback is a morphism of mixed Hodge structures; if $V$ is smooth and projective, the cohomology of $V/G$ is pure of the indicated degree.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "If a finite group $G$ acts algebraically on a projective variety $V$, the quotient map $q:V\\to V/G$ induces $$\\label{p4:finite-quotient}\n q^*:H^i(V/G,\\mathbb Q)\\xrightarrow{\\;\\sim\\;}H^i(V,\\mathbb Q)^G.$$ The assertion remains true after extending coefficients.         "anchor": "stmt-p4-bundle-blowup-supply",
        "exact_quote": "If the full cohomology of a smooth projective variety $S$ is CM-supplied, the same holds for every projective bundle over $S$. In both assertions finite correspondence supplies for the inputs give finite supplies for the output in each degree.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-p4-fermat-supply",
        "exact_quote": "For integers $n\\ge2$ and $a\\ge0$, let $$F_n^a=\\{x_0^n+\\cdots+x_{a+1}^n=0\\}\n       \\subset\\mathbb P^{a+1}_\\mathbb C.$$ For every degree $q$, there are finitely many CM abelian varieties $B_\\nu$, integers $j_\\nu$, and rational algebraic correspondences $\\Gamma_\\nu\\in\\operatorname{CH}^{\\dim B_\\nu+j_\\nu}(B_\\nu\\times F_n^a)_\\mathbb Q$ such that the sum of their maps $$\\bigoplus_\\nu H^{q-2j_\\nu}(B_\\nu,\\mathbb Q)(-j_\\nu)\n \\xrightarrow{\\;\\sum_\\nu(\\Gamma_\\nu)_*\\;}\n H^q(F_n^a,\\mathbb Q)$$ is surjective. One may take every $B_\\nu$ to be a power of the CM abelian variety $\\operatorname{Jac}(F_n^1)$, allowing its zeroth power, a point. In dimension zero the finite reduced scheme $F_n^0$ is supplied component by component by points.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p4-fermat-supply",
          "exact_quote": "For integers $n\\ge2$ and $a\\ge0$, let $$F_n^a=\\{x_0^n+\\cdots+x_{a+1}^n=0\\}\n       \\subset\\mathbb P^{a+1}_\\mathbb C.$$ For every degree $q$, there are finitely many CM abelian varieties $B_\\nu$, integers $j_\\nu$, and rational algebraic correspondences $\\Gamma_\\nu\\in\\operatorname{CH}^{\\dim B_\\nu+j_\\nu}(B_\\nu\\times F_n^a)_\\mathbb Q$ such that the sum of their maps $$\\bigoplus_\\nu H^{q-2j_\\nu}(B_\\nu,\\mathbb Q)(-j_\\nu)\n \\xrightarrow{\\;\\sum_\\nu(\\Gamma_\\nu)_*\\;}\n H^q(F_n^a,\\mathbb Q)$$ is surjective. One may take every $B_\\nu$ to be a power of the CM abelian variety $\\operatorname{Jac}(F_n^1)$, allowing its zeroth power, a point. In dimension zero the finite reduced scheme $F_n^0$ is supplied component by component by points.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.4"

        "anchor": "stmt-p4-cyclic-determinant",
        "exact_quote": "Let $q:C\\to B$ be a connected cyclic cover of smooth connected projective complex curves, with group $\\Delta$ of order $m\\ge2$.         "source_id": "PM-032.4"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p4-abelian-determinants",
        "exact_quote": "Let a finite abelian group $P$ act faithfully on a smooth connected projective complex curve $C$, and put $B=C/P$. For every character $\\chi:P\\to k^\\times$, the line $D(H^1(C,k)_\\chi)$ is CM-supplied. This includes the zero character spaces. For the trivial character the line is generated by a rational algebraic cycle class.*\n\n*For $\\chi\\ne1$, let $r_\\chi$ be the number of branch points of $C/\\ker\\chi\\to B$. Then $$\\dim_k H^1(C,k)_\\chi=2g(B)+r_\\chi-2.$$ The number $r_\\chi$ counts precisely the original branch points whose inertia is not killed by $\\chi$.*\n\n*For a fixed $P$, the CM sources for all these lines can be taken to be powers of one fixed product of Fermat-curve Jacobians depending only on $P$, not on the complex structure of $C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p4-generic-tensors",
        "exact_quote": "For $a\\ge0$ and $t\\in H_\\mathbb Q^{\\otimes 2a}$, let $$Z_t=\\{s\\in\\mathcal T_\\phi:\n       t_\\mathbb C\\in F^a(H_\\mathbb C^{\\otimes 2a})_s\\},\\qquad\n \\mathcal T_\\phi^{\\mathrm{Hg}}\n =\\mathcal T_\\phi\\setminus\n   \\bigcup_{\\substack{a\\ge0,\\ t\\in H_\\mathbb Q^{\\otimes 2a}\\\\\n                       Z_t\\ne\\mathcal T_\\phi}}Z_t.$$ Each $Z_t$ is closed complex analytic. The complement $\\mathcal T_\\phi^{\\mathrm{Hg}}$ is nonempty and is the complement of a countable union of proper closed analytic subsets. At a point in this complement, every rational balanced tensor is balanced throughout $\\mathcal T_\\phi$.*\n\n*Here $H_s$ denotes $H_\\mathbb Q$ with the Hodge structure at $s$. Let $J_s=\\operatorname{Hdg}(H_s)$ and let $L$ be the rational Zariski closure of $\\Gamma$ in the symplectic group of $H_\\mathbb Q$. At every $s\\in\\mathcal T_\\phi^{\\mathrm{Hg}}$ one has $L^\\circ\\subset J_s$, and the whole $\\Gamma$ normalizes $J_s$.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-p4-twist-model",
        "exact_quote": "For a nontrivial $t$, the character space is the space of crossed homomorphisms $$\\label{p4:cocycles}\n z(uv)=z(u)+t(u)z(v),\n \\qquad z(u)\\sim z(u)+(1-t(u))b\\quad(b\\in k).$$ Use generators $a_j,b_j$ for the handles and positively based meridians $g_i$, with relation $\\prod_j[a_j,b_j]\\prod_i g_i=1$. Values of $z$ on these generators satisfy just the crossed-homomorphism relation on this word, followed by the one-dimensional coboundary quotient.*\n\n\n*Let $\\alpha$ be an embedded arc between two active punctures. A twist about the boundary of its two-puncture disk acts, in adapted coordinates and after choosing one of the two twist conventions, by $$\\label{p4:pair-twist}\n T_\\alpha=1+u_\\alpha\\ell_\\alpha,\\qquad\n u_\\alpha=(-t_i,1),\\qquad\n \\ell_\\alpha(z)=(1-t_j)z(g_i)+(t_i-1)z(g_j),$$ with $u_\\alpha$ supported on these two meridians. Its exceptional eigenvalue is $t_it_j$. A different choice of lift can multiply this formula by a finite-order scalar.*\n\n*For a nontrivial such transformation, its fixed hyperplane is the Hermitian orthogonal complement of its image direction. If a collection of these directions has connected nonorthogonality graph and their fixed hyperplanes have zero intersection, the group they generate acts irreducibly. These statements apply also to ordinary transvections arising from simple closed curves with trivial $t$-monodromy.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-p4-twist-model",
          "exact_quote": "For a nontrivial $t$, the character space is the space of crossed homomorphisms $$\\label{p4:cocycles}\n z(uv)=z(u)+t(u)z(v),\n \\qquad z(u)\\sim z(u)+(1-t(u))b\\quad(b\\in k).$$ Use generators $a_j,b_j$ for the handles and positively based meridians $g_i$, with relation $\\prod_j[a_j,b_j]\\prod_i g_i=1$. Values of $z$ on these generators satisfy just the crossed-homomorphism relation on this word, followed by the one-dimensional coboundary quotient.*\n\n\n*Let $\\alpha$ be an embedded arc between two active punctures. A twist about the boundary of its two-puncture disk acts, in adapted coordinates and after choosing one of the two twist conventions, by $$\\label{p4:pair-twist}\n T_\\alpha=1+u_\\alpha\\ell_\\alpha,\\qquad\n u_\\alpha=(-t_i,1),\\qquad\n \\ell_\\alpha(z)=(1-t_j)z(g_i)+(t_i-1)z(g_j),$$ with $u_\\alpha$ supported on these two meridians. Its exceptional eigenvalue is $t_it_j$. A different choice of lift can multiply this formula by a finite-order scalar.*\n\n*For a nontrivial such transformation, its fixed hyperplane is the Hermitian orthogonal complement of its image direction. If a collection of these directions has connected nonorthogonality graph and their fixed hyperplanes have zero intersection, the group they generate acts irreducibly. These statements apply also to ordinary transvections arising from simple closed curves with trivial $t$-monodromy.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For a nontrivial $t$, the character space is the space of crossed homomorphisms $$\\label{p4:cocycles}\n z(uv)=z(u)+t(u)z(v),\n \\qquad z(u)\\sim z(u)+(1-t(u))b\\quad(b\\in k).$$ Use generators $a_j,b_j$ for the handles and positively based meridians $g_i$, with relation $\\prod_j[a_j,b_j]\\prod_i g_i=1$. Values of $z$ on these generators satisfy just the crossed-homomorphism relation on this word, followed by the one-dimensional coboundary quotient.*\n\n\n*Let $\\alpha$ be an embedded arc between two active punctures. A twist about the boundary of its two-puncture disk acts, in adapted coordinates and after choosing one of the two twist conventions, by $$\\label{p4:pair-twist}\n T_\\alpha=1+u_\\alpha\\ell_\\alpha,\\qquad\n u_\\alpha=(-t_i,1),\\qquad\n \\ell_\\alpha(z)=(1-t_j)z(g_i)+(t_i-1)z(g_j),$$ with $u_\\alpha$ supported on these two meridians.         "exact_quote": "Place the active punctures in an ordered disk, away from the handle subsurface. If $r\\ge3$, the arc can avoid the interior of the standard arc from $2$ to $3$.         "anchor": "stmt-p4-sphere-projections",
        "exact_quote": "At every $s\\in\\mathcal T_\\phi^{\\mathrm{Hg}}$ the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *If $n\\ge3$, then either $M_\\chi$ is finite and the Hodge action on its rational scalar orbit is toric, or $$M_\\chi^\\circ=\n     \\begin{cases}\n      \\operatorname{Sp}(W_\\chi),&\\chi^2=1,\\\\\n      \\operatorname{SL}(W_\\chi),&\\chi^2\\ne1.\n     \\end{cases}$$ In the infinite case the derived Hodge projection is the same standard group.*\n\n2.  *In dimensions at most two every non-toric character space has dimension two and has standard derived Hodge projection $\\operatorname{SL}_2$.*\n\n*Here the symplectic group is for the restricted polarization on the order-two character space.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "For $P\\in\\mathcal U_{d,m}$, $X_P$ is a smooth projective complete intersection of dimension $d$, connected if $d\\geq1$. For $d=0$, $X_P$ is a $G_{m,n}$-torsor of reduced points.*\n\n*The open $\\mathcal U_{d,m}$ is nonempty and connected, and the $X_P$ form a smooth projective family over it with its phase action.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "For $P\\in\\mathcal U_{d,m}$, $X_P$ is a smooth projective complete intersection of dimension $d$, connected if $d\\geq1$. For $d=0$, $X_P$ is a $G_{m,n}$-torsor of reduced points.*\n\n*The open $\\mathcal U_{d,m}$ is nonempty and connected, and the $X_P$ form a smooth projective family over it with its phase action.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.4"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-evaluation",
        "exact_quote": "Choose distinct $a_1,\\ldots,a_m\\in\\mathbb C$, $m\\geq3$, and put $$P_j=\\{(f(a_1),\\ldots,f(a_m)):\\deg f\\leq j\\}\n \\quad(1\\leq j\\leq m-2),\\qquad C=X_{P_1}.$$ Coordinate multiplication defines a finite surjective morphism $$\\mu_j:C^j\\to X_{P_j},\\qquad\n (x^{(1)},\\ldots,x^{(j)})\\mapsto\n       [\\prod_{\\nu=1}^j x_i^{(\\nu)}]_{i=1}^m,$$ which identifies the target with $$C^j\\big/\\left(\\ker(G_{m,n}^j\\xrightarrow{\\prod}G_{m,n})\n                  \\rtimes\\mathfrak S_j\\right).$$ For $b\\ne0$, pullback identifies $$H^j(X_{P_j},\\overline{\\mathbb Q})_b\\simeq\\bigwedge^j V_b,\\qquad\n V_b=H^1(C,\\overline{\\mathbb Q})_b,$$ with the exterior power embedded by antisymmetrization.",
        "source_id": "PM-032.4"
      },

        "exact_quote": "For $P\\in\\mathcal U_{d,m}$, invariant cohomology is the pullback of $H^*(\\mathbb P(P),\\overline{\\mathbb Q})$ and is spanned by algebraic hyperplane classes.         "anchor": "stmt-thm-gale",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P\\in\\mathcal U_{d,m}$, set $$Q=P^\\perp\\quad\\text{for }\\sum_i u_i v_i,\\qquad d'=m-d-2.$$ Then $Q\\in\\mathcal U_{d',m}$, and $$f_P:X_P\\times X_Q\\longrightarrow F_n^{m-2},\\qquad\n ([x_i],[y_i])\\longmapsto[x_i y_i]$$ is an algebraic morphism.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-supply",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P\\in\\mathcal U_{d,m}$. For each $S\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,m\\}$ with $s=|S|\\geq d+2$, put $$P_S=\\operatorname{pr}_S P,\\quad Q_S=P_S^\\perp,\\quad Y_S=X_{Q_S},\n \\quad h_S=s-d-2.$$ There is a rational algebraic correspondence from $F_n^{s-2}\\times Y_S$ to $X_P$, with degree shift $-2h_S$, whose image after scalar extension contains $W_{P,b}$ for every character with support $S$.*\n\n*Together with hyperplane cycles from a point, these finitely many maps span the full rational cohomology of $X_P$.         "exact_quote": "Let $X_P$ have dimension $d$ and codimension $c=m-d-1\\in\\{1,2\\}$, and write $S=S(b)$ for the support of a character $b$.*\n\n1.  *If $c=1$, every nontrivial character $b$ with $W_{P,b}\\ne0$ has full support, and its dual source $Y_S$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:supply\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:supply\">32</a> is finite.*\n\n2.  *If $c=2$, every nontrivial character $b$ with $W_{P,b}\\ne0$ has support of size $m$ or $m-1$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Rational Hodge conjecture** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For a smooth projective complex variety $X$, the rational Hodge conjecture asserts that the cycle class map $$\operatorname{CH}^p(X)_\mathbb Q\longrightarrow H^{2p}(X,\mathbb Q)\cap H^{p,p}(X,\mathbb C)$$ is surjective for every $p$.
- **Tensor Hodge-generic cover locus** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $\mathcal T_\phi^{\mathrm{Hg}}$ consist of the parameters $s$ with the following property: for every $a\geq0$, any rational tensor in $H_\mathbb Q^{\otimes 2a}$ of type $(a,a)$ at $s$ has that type throughout $\mathcal T_\phi$.
- **CM abelian variety** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A CM abelian variety here means one whose degree-one Hodge group is a torus; a point is allowed.
- **CM-supplied subspace** ([source](#sec:shared)): A subspace of $H^d(X,k)$ is *CM-supplied* if it lies in $\mathcal C^d(X)\otimes_\mathbb Qk$.
- **CM correspondence-image span** ([source](#sec:shared)): For a smooth projective variety $X$, define $$\mathcal C^d(X)= \sum_{B,j,\Gamma}\operatorname{im}\!\left( \Gamma_*:H^{d-2j}(B,\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow H^d(X,\mathbb Q)\right), \qquad \Gamma\in\operatorname{CH}^{\dim B+j}(B\times X)_\mathbb Q,$$ where $B$ ranges over CM abelian varieties, including a point, and $j$ ranges over integers for which the displayed groups and cycles exist.
- **Admissible degree-one subspace** ([source](#sec:shared)): A subspace $W\subset H^1(X,k)$ is *admissible* if it is the image of a $k$-linear idempotent whose every scalar conjugate preserves the Hodge decomposition after extension to $\mathbb C$.
- **Admissible map** ([source](#sec:shared)): A map between such spaces, or between finite external direct sums of them, is admissible if it preserves the Hodge decomposition after every scalar conjugation.
- **Pair tensor** ([source](#sec:shared)): A *pair tensor* between $W,W'$ is a tensor in $W\otimes W'$ inducing an isomorphism $W^*\to W'$, whose every scalar conjugate has type $(1,1)$.
- **Character determinant line** ([source](#sec:shared)): For $r=\dim W$, put $$D(W)=\bigwedge^rW\subset W^{\otimes r}\subset H^r(X^r,k), \qquad D(0)=k$$ on separate tensor slots, with $D(0)$ carried by a point.
- **Degree-one Hodge group** ([source](#sec:shared)): For a polarizable rational weight-one Hodge structure $H$ of types $(1,0),(0,1)$, write $\operatorname{Hdg}(H)$ for the smallest rational algebraic subgroup of $\operatorname{GL}(H)$ containing the Hodge circle.
- **Balanced tensor** ([source](#sec:shared)): A tensor in $H^{\otimes 2a}$ is *balanced* when it has type $(a,a)$.
- **Coordinate-general subspace and root cover** ([source](#stmt-def-root)): Let $m,n\geq2$ and $0\leq d\leq m-2$. A subspace $P\subset\mathbb C^m$ of dimension $d+1$ is *coordinate-general* if every $d+1$ coordinate functionals restrict to a basis of $P^*$. Let $\mathcal U_{d,m}\subset\operatorname{Gr}(d+1,m)$ be this open locus. For $P\in\mathcal U_{d,m}$, define $$X_P=\{[x_i]\in\mathbb P^{m-1}:(x_1^n,\ldots,x_m^n)\in P\},\qquad G_{m,n}=(\mu_n)^m/\mu_n,$$ where the denominator is diagonal. The phase action preserves $X_P$, and $q_P:X_P\to\mathbb P(P)$, $[x_i]\mapsto[x_i^n]$, is its coordinate-power map.
- **Character support** ([source](#pm-h-24)): Put $S(b)=\{i:b_i\ne0\}$, $s(b)=\|S(b)\|$, and $W_{P,b}=H^d(X_P,\overline{\mathbb Q})_b$.
- **Pure and mixed character spaces** ([source](#pm-h-19)): For a nonzero complex character space, *pure* and *mixed* mean respectively that just one, or both, of its $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$ parts are nonzero.
- **Non-toric character space** ([source](#p4:sphere)): A character space is *non-toric* if the image of $J^{\mathrm{der}}$ on it is nontrivial.
- **Normalized branch-configuration space** ([source](#pm-h-28)): Let $$M_m=\{(z_4,\ldots,z_m)\in(\mathbb A^1\setminus\{0,1\})^{m-3}: z_i\ne z_j\},$$ with $M_3$ a point.
- **Tate convention** ([source](#sec:shared)): Our Tate convention is $V(r)^{p,q}=V^{p+r,q+r}$, so $V(r)$ has weight $\operatorname{wt}(V)-2r$.

Let $\\mathcal U_{d,m}\\subset\\operatorname{Gr}(d+1,m)$ be this open locus. For $P\\in\\mathcal U_{d,m}$, define $$X_P=\\{[x_i]\\in\\mathbb P^{m-1}:(x_1^n,\\ldots,x_m^n)\\in P\\},\\qquad\n G_{m,n}=(\\mu_n)^m/\\mu_n,$$ where the denominator is diagonal. The phase action preserves $X_P$, and $q_P:X_P\\to\\mathbb P(P)$, $[x_i]\\mapsto[x_i^n]$, is its coordinate-power map.",
          "source_id": "PM-032.4"
        }

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-diagonal-coordinate-count | m | = | $d+c+1$ | Degree, dimension, and codimension are fixed as in the main diagonal theorem. |
| q-cyclic-character-rank | \dim_k V_\chi | = | $2g+r-2$ | The character is faithful; genus and reduced branching belong to the quotient base. |
| q-abelian-character-rank | \dim_k H^1(C,k)_\chi | = | $2g(B)+r_\chi-2$ | Active branching is measured in the faithful cyclic quotient, rather than by all branch points of the original cover. |
| q-sphere-character-rank | \dim_k W_\chi | = | $r-2$ | Inactive punctures are discarded; the remaining local multipliers are active. |
| q-four-point-first-hodge-multiplicity | First Hodge multiplicity in the fixed character convention | = | $S-1$ | The convention fixes which multiplicity is listed first; inversion exchanges the multiplicities. |
| q-four-point-second-hodge-multiplicity | Second Hodge multiplicity in the fixed character convention | = | $3-S$ | The two values are multiplicities of weight-one Hodge components, rather than Hodge bidegrees. |
| q-diagonal-variety-dimension | Dimension of X_A | = | $m-c-1$ | The coefficient matrix has the stated rank and its equations have the common stated degree. |
| q-diagonal-intersection-degree | Degree of X_A | = | $n^c$ | Every defining equation has the common degree and the equations form a complete intersection. |
| q-support-character-rank | \dim_{\overline{\mathbb Q}} W_{P,b} | = | $\binom{s(b)-2}{d}$ | The binomial value is zero when the support is below the stated threshold. |
| q-gale-dual-dimension | d' | = | $m-d-2$ | The dual is formed with the ordinary bilinear pairing, including finite endpoints. |
| q-supported-dual-dimension | h_S | = | $s-d-2$ | Support has sufficient size for the contributing character space. |
| q-gale-correspondence-shift | Cohomological degree shift of the forward Gale correspondence | = | $-2h_S$ | The source retains its Fermat factor and the correspondence acts on the whole indicated rational source degree. |
| q-dual-boundary-fiber-size | Number of reduced points in the missing-coordinate dual source | = | $n^{m-2}$ | The missing coordinate is omitted and the boundary fiber is reduced. |
| q-coordinate-root-cover-degree | Degree of the coordinate-root parameter cover | = | $n^{m-1}$ | The first coordinate root is fixed and the remaining torus coordinates undergo the power map. |
| q-dual-curve-riemann-hurwitz | 2g(C)-2 | = | $n^{m-2}\bigl(m(n-1)-2n\bigr)$ | Every labelled branch point has the indicated inertia order. |
| q-three-branch-fermat-genus | Genus of the three-branch Fermat dual curve | = | $(n-1)(n-2)/2$ | The branch-configuration base is a point. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-companion-operations** [claim](#stmt-comp-tools) / [proof](#sec:shared): Use the recorded companion algebraic operations and balanced-tensor stabilizer statements. -> Algebraic closure operations, toric source factors, and the tensor stabilizer.
- **step-saturated-criterion** [claim](#stmt-comp-saturated) / [proof](#sec:shared): Apply the companion saturated determinant criterion once its block and singleton-supply hypotheses are established. -> Balanced-tensor algebraicity on all powers, stable under every CM companion.
- **step-product-transfer** [claim](#stmt-comp-transfer) / [proof](#sec:shared): Lift Hodge classes through whole-degree correspondence maps from sources satisfying the finite-product Hodge hypothesis. -> Rational Hodge conjecture for products of target varieties.
- **step-finite-quotient** [claim](#stmt-lem-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quotient): Combine proper base change, exact averaging, and Leray; use mixed-Hodge strictness for purity. -> Quotient cohomology equals invariant cohomology.
- **step-bundle-blowup** [claim](#stmt-p4-bundle-blowup-supply) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-bundle-blowup-supply): Express bundle and blowup cohomology through algebraic pullbacks, Chern powers, and exceptional pushforwards. -> Finite full-cohomology supplies for bundles and blowups.
- **step-fermat-supply** [claim](#stmt-p4-fermat-supply) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-fermat-supply): Prove the Fermat-curve Jacobian is CM, resolve coordinate-product domination by a blowup, and induct using surjective pushforward. -> Full Fermat cohomology supplied by powers of a fixed CM Jacobian.
- **step-cyclic-determinant** [claim](#stmt-p4-cyclic-determinant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-cyclic-determinant): Isolate the alternating character line on a finite quotient; localize to the distinguished Abel fiber; dominate it by Fermat bundles or projective spaces; detect with weights and return by a correspondence. -> CM supply of the faithful character determinant from fixed Fermat sources.
- **step-abelian-determinants** [claim](#stmt-p4-abelian-determinants) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-abelian-determinants): Pass each nontrivial character to its faithful cyclic quotient and pull back; use the polarization determinant for the invariant character. -> All abelian character determinants with uniform fixed CM source product.
- **step-marked-generic-tensors** [claim](#stmt-p4-generic-tensors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-generic-tensors): Remove proper analytic tensor loci by countability and Baire; use the integral polarization norm to make global tensor orbits finite. -> Nonempty tensor Hodge-generic locus with connected monodromy containment and normalization.
- **step-rank-one-classification** [claim](#stmt-p4-cover-rank-one) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-cover-rank-one): Reduce the semisimple image to a simple ideal and classify root-vector rank-one representations by their weight reflections. -> Standard special linear or symplectic derived image.
- **step-cocycle-twists** [claim](#stmt-p4-twist-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-twist-model): Compute crossed homomorphisms and disk-twist displacement; use polarization orthogonality to propagate invariant directions. -> Rank-one twist formulas and an irreducibility test.
- **step-connected-irreducibility** [claim](#stmt-p4-connected-irreducibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-connected-irreducibility): Analyze reductive connected isotypic summands and show rank-one generators cannot permute the nontrivial summands. -> Irreducibility of the identity-component action.
- **step-winding-arcs** [claim](#stmt-p4-winding-arcs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-winding-arcs): Smooth the based access-path concatenation inside a vertex disk using noncrossing joins and separated handle regions. -> Embedded winding arcs realizing the required fixed-point equations.
- **step-positive-genus-projections** [claim](#stmt-p4-positive-projections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-positive-projections): Treat unramified, multiple-active-point, and paired-active-point cases with handle transvections and winding arcs; exclude pure type using a nontrivial unipotent isometry. -> Mixed scalar translates and standard derived character projections.
- **step-sphere-determinant-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-p4-sphere-determinant-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-sphere-determinant-finite): Use integral unit determinants and polarization at all embeddings; use invariant lattices in definite scalar orbits. -> Finite determinant image and scalar-orbit finiteness criteria.
- **step-finite-sphere-purity** [claim](#stmt-p4-sphere-finite-pure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-sphere-finite-pure): Kill finite monodromy on a finite étale configuration cover, extend bounded period coordinates to a compactification, and contradict constant periods by the mixed-character deformation differential. -> Pure scalar translates and toric rational orbit action.
- **step-infinite-sphere-rank-one** [claim](#stmt-p4-sphere-connected-irreducible) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-sphere-connected-irreducible): Use arc irreducibility, isotypic analysis, definite-orbit exclusion, and adjoint eigenspace projection to produce a rank-one nilpotent. -> Connected irreducibility and a rank-one Lie element.
- **step-sphere-projections** [claim](#stmt-p4-sphere-projections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-sphere-projections): Apply rank-one classification, distinguish the symplectic character case through twist eigenvalues, and treat low ranks directly in reductive Hodge groups. -> Standard sphere projections and unique supporting simple ideals.
- **step-four-point-signatures** [claim](#stmt-p4-sphere-four-signatures) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-sphere-four-signatures): Count cyclic eigendifferentials by branch regularity and the degree condition at infinity, then invert exponents for the opposite space. -> The two Hodge multiplicities and pure-orientation classification.
- **step-sphere-links** [claim](#stmt-p4-sphere-links) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-sphere-links): Intertwine projective mapping-class actions through the shared ideal; compare active pair and triple twists; align pure orientations through the fixed four-point tuple relation. -> Admissible within-cover standard or polarized-dual identifications.
- **step-mixed-links** [claim](#stmt-p4-mixed-links) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-mixed-links): Compare Hodge-circle spectra on the intertwiner line at every scalar conjugate and on invariant alternating-form lines. -> Admissible Hodge intertwiners and symplectic pair tensors.
- **step-cover-saturation** [claim](#stmt-p4-cover-saturation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-mixed-links-2): Transport curve summands to Jacobian slots, collect toric orbits, group spaces by supporting ideals, establish independent standard actions, and supply representative determinants. -> Saturated data for the generic cover Jacobian.
- **step-cover-powers** [claim](#stmt-p4-abelian-covers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p4-mixed-links-2): Apply saturated data after adjoining arbitrary CM factors, then use curve–Jacobian and point correspondences to transfer to curve products. -> The cover theorem for all Jacobian powers and indicated mixed curve products.
- **step-exact-smooth-locus** [claim](#stmt-prop-smooth-locus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-smooth-locus): Use freeness of the coordinate-power ring map for complete intersection dimension, then the Jacobian criterion and complementary column dependencies. -> Exact minor criterion for smoothness and coordinate generality.
- **step-ambient-cohomology** [claim](#stmt-lem-ambient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ambient): Choose a smooth partial-intersection flag; apply Lefschetz successively and Poincaré duality; show hyperplane powers are nonzero by degree. -> Ambient nonmiddle cohomology and connectedness.
- **step-root-geometry** [claim](#stmt-lem-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-geometry): Use local coordinate-root charts for relative smoothness and normal crossings, phase orbits for the quotient, and Vandermonde evaluation planes for nonempty connected parameters. -> Smooth projective root-cover family and finite endpoint torsors.
- **step-evaluation-quotient** [claim](#stmt-lem-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-evaluation): Multiply binary linear forms, use ampleness for finiteness, identify the phase-permutation quotient by normality, and account for degree-one permutation signs. -> Evaluation character cohomology as exterior powers.
- **step-character-support** [claim](#stmt-prop-characters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-characters): Compute active-point curve rank at evaluation planes, take exterior powers, transport rank on connected parameters, and identify support projections as finite phase quotients. -> Support-dependent middle character ranks and support pullback isomorphisms.
- **step-perfect-gale** [claim](#stmt-thm-gale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-gale): Verify bilinear complementary minors and coordinate multiplication; compute the exterior coproduct at evaluation planes; transport pairing rank; handle finite endpoints directly. -> Perfect full-support Gale tensor at every smooth parameter.
- **step-forward-gale** [claim](#stmt-prop-supply) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-supply): Contract the perfect tensor with opposite-character Poincaré cohomology using a rational graph cycle and compose with support projection pullback. -> Finite whole-degree rational correspondence supply of all target cohomology.
- **step-single-dual-curve** [claim](#stmt-lem-one-curve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-one-curve): Combine support rank thresholds with the codimension relation and identify missing-coordinate duals by zero extension. -> Finite dual sources or the single connected full-support curve.
- **step-branch-product** [claim](#stmt-prop-parameter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-parameter): Use Plücker relations and an explicit normalized row frame to construct inverse regular maps between coefficient parameters and configuration-times-torus parameters. -> Smooth surjective branch projection and global product decomposition.
- **step-fixed-root-family** [claim](#stmt-prop-cover-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cover-family): Take torus coordinate roots; identify families by scaling; compute Kummer degree and local meridian monodromies to fix the full cover datum. -> Phase-equivariant family identifications and every ordered branch configuration of the fixed cover.
- **step-sphere-isotopy** [claim](#stmt-lem-sphere-isotopy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sphere-isotopy): Extend a disk homeomorphism across annular complements, then apply the Alexander trick to the supported map and the complementary disk. -> Isotopy to the identity with freely moving labels.
- **step-based-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-based) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-based): Use connectedness of the unbranched total family and lift normalized configuration paths with the transported based point; extend through the fixed ramification charts. -> Every marked cover filtration reached by root-family transport.
- **step-algebraic-tensor-genericity** [claim](#stmt-lem-tensor-generic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tensor-generic): Apply algebraicity and finite-image closedness of integral tensor Hodge loci; use Baire to show nonglobal tensor loci are proper; remove their countable union. -> Algebraic very-general locus with pathwise global Hodge tensors.
- **step-good-dual-curves** [claim](#stmt-cor-good-curves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-good-curves): Combine algebraic tensor genericity with the based marked comparison, apply the cover theorem, and pull proper exceptional loci back through the surjective branch map. -> Uniform algebraic exceptional union for dual curve powers with all CM companions.
- **step-diagonal-powers-assembly** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-good-curves-2): Compose Fermat CM source maps and dual curve–Jacobian maps with Gale correspondences, track Tate shifts on whole degrees, and verify finite-product source Hodge hypotheses before transfer. -> Rational Hodge conjecture for every diagonal self-power at the same permitted parameter.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-perfect-gale-pairing**: At a coordinate-general parameter, coordinate multiplication pulls a full-support Fermat character line back to a tensor inducing an isomorphism between the complementary character spaces.
- **invariant-complementary-wedge-bases**: The exterior coproduct pairs complementary increasing wedge bases bijectively, with nonzero signed coefficients.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-finite-check-as-hodge-proof**: Treat agreement of exterior coproduct matrices as certification of the rational Hodge conjecture on all self-powers. -> The matrix check establishes a finite algebraic component of the argument; it does not establish algebraicity of Hodge classes, CM correspondence supply, or the genericity of a parameter.
- **anti-use-hermitian-gale-complement**: Replace the bilinear orthogonal complement by a Hermitian orthogonal complement. -> The argument that coordinate multiplication lands on the Fermat hypersurface uses bilinear orthogonality, and the parameterwise correspondence requires algebraic dependence.
- **anti-use-arbitrary-character-support**: Apply the full-support perfect tensor directly to a character with missing coordinates. -> The stated Fermat character line and complementary pairing require full support in the selected coordinate set.
- **anti-use-drop-exterior-signs**: Replace every complementary wedge coefficient by a positive coefficient. -> This changes the exterior coproduct: exchanging degree-one factors carries the alternating sign.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The selected executable result is the finite exterior coproduct calculation in the proof of the perfect Gale tensor, rather than the Hodge conjecture itself. The source states: “Each wedge uses increasing order.” It then pairs complementary wedge bases bijectively with nonzero coefficients. The geometric extension to every smooth parameter, CM supply, and Hodge-genericity remain outside this finite check.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with integer N and d; optional case label.
# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= d <= N <= 8; integers are not booleans.
# OUTPUTS: JSON coefficient matrix in lexicographic increasing wedge bases.
# TIME: Output-sized construction with quadratic sign work per entry.
# SPACE: The coefficient matrix and increasing subset bases.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python integers.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.
import json
import sys
from itertools import combinations

obj = json.load(sys.stdin)
N = obj['N']
d = obj['d']
if type(N) is not int or type(d) is not int or not (0 <= d <= N <= 8):
    raise ValueError('invalid exterior dimensions')
rows = list(combinations(range(N), d))
columns = list(combinations(range(N), N - d))
matrix = []
for I in rows:
    row = []
    for J in columns:
        if set(I).isdisjoint(J):
            inversions = sum(i > j for i in I for j in J)
            coefficient = (-1 if inversions % 2 else 1)
        else:
            coefficient = 0
        row.append(coefficient)
    matrix.append(row)
print(json.dumps(matrix, separators=(',', ':')))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The exact smooth-locus criterion supports a symbolic validator for diagonal complete-intersection presentations: verify the coefficient matrix rank and reject a presentation whenever a maximal column minor vanishes. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The coordinate-root construction describes smooth projective varieties and normal-crossing boundaries. It supplies no evolution rule, collision model, or simulation update procedure. |
| tools | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The branch-map formulas support an exact symbolic normalization tool that converts a smooth labelled coefficient presentation into normalized ordered branch coordinates. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The support-rank formula gives a finite exact calculation of the dimension of a nontrivial character eigenspace. Its support threshold also detects characters whose middle-degree contribution vanishes. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The character decomposition organizes cohomological summands, but the paper specifies no storage representation, update operation, or access contract for a computational data structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Fermat supply is constructed through algebraic correspondences and CM abelian varieties. It provides no hardware execution architecture or accelerator resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite abelian deck datum specifies geometric covers and their local monodromies. It supplies neither a cryptographic hardness assumption nor a security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Balanced tensors are used to characterize Hodge groups in polarized cohomology. The tensor stabilizer statement supplies no learning objective, training procedure, or statistical guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gale tensor gives a perfect pairing between complementary character spaces. This rank assertion does not define an objective function, feasible optimization problem, or optimization method. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Correspondence transfer concerns cohomology of finite products of smooth projective varieties. Its product hypothesis supplies no distributed communication, consistency, or fault-recovery contract. |
| other (Symbolic algebraic geometry) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper gives a concrete geometric construction: take the bilinear orthogonal complement of a coordinate-general plane and multiply root coordinates to obtain an algebraic morphism to a Fermat hypersurface. This supports symbolic construction of the source, target, and map; the cohomological perfectness assertion remains a mathematical theorem rather than a general cycle-computation procedure. |
